In the next section, we applied the proposed MCDM approach to a case study.

Case study

The classification of renewable energy sources is critical in determining the most viable options for sustainable energy production. In this case study, a model with four alternatives and four criteria is evaluated by three decision makers. This model aims to provide a structured approach to selecting the most suitable renewable energy source based on multiple factors.

Alternatives.

Hydro Energy \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\Xi\:}}_{1}$$\end{document}: Utilizing the energy of flowing water, typically from rivers or dams, to generate electricity through hydroelectric power plants.

Wind Energy \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\Xi\:}}_{2}$$\end{document}: Generating electricity through wind turbines that convert kinetic energy from wind into electrical power.

Solar Energy \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\Xi\:}}_{3}$$\end{document}: Harvesting energy from sunlight using photovoltaic panels or solar thermal collectors.

Biomass Energy \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\Xi\:}}_{4}$$\end{document}: Producing energy by burning organic materials such as wood, agricultural residues, and other biological waste to generate heat or electricity.

Criteria.

Cost \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\Psi\:}}_{1}$$\end{document}: This criterion evaluates the total investment, operation, and maintenance costs associated with each renewable energy source.

Environmental Impact \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\Psi\:}}_{2}$$\end{document}: This assesses the ecological footprint of each energy source, including emissions, habitat disruption, and resource depletion.

Reliability \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\Psi\:}}_{3}$$\end{document}: Evaluates the consistency and dependability of the energy supply from each source, considering factors like weather dependency and availability.

Scalability \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\Psi\:}}_{4}$$\end{document}: This criterion examines the potential for each energy source to be scaled up to meet increasing energy demands without significant loss of efficiency or excessive costs.

Decision makers (experts).

Environmental Scientist \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\Theta\:}}_{1}$$\end{document}: An expert in environmental sciences with a focus on the impact of energy production on ecosystems and biodiversity.

Economist \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\Theta\:}}_{2}$$\end{document}: A professional economist specializing in the cost analysis and economic feasibility of renewable energy projects.

Energy Engineer \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\Theta\:}}_{3}$$\end{document}: An experienced energy engineer with a background in designing and implementing renewable energy systems.

Each decision maker evaluates the four alternatives based on the four criteria. The evaluation involves assigning scores to each alternative for each criterion. The assessment values of alternatives are provided in the form of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:(p,q,r)-$$\end{document}SFNs and listed in Tables 1, 2 and 3. The layout of the proposed model is presented in Fig. 5.

\usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\Theta\:}}_{1}$$\end{document}Alternatives \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} The decision matrix provided by energy engineer \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\Theta\:}}_{3}$$\end{document}

Scalability and Reliability are typically considered benefit-type criteria because they focus on the positive aspects of a system. Environmental Impact and Cost are usually considered cost-type criteria because they involve evaluating the negative aspects associated with a service. Thus, using Eq. (14) to normalize the decision matrices provided by decision makers. The normalized decision matrices are listed in Tables 4, 5 and 6.

the flowchart of proposed case study.

We assume that the weights assigned to the decision makers are distributed as follows: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:35\%$$\end{document} for Environmental Scientist, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:30\%$$\end{document} for Economist, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:35\%$$\end{document} for Energy Engineer. Nevertheless, these weights remain flexible and subject to adjustment based on the specific needs or circumstances of the decision-making process. Following Step 6, we utilized the information provided in Tables 4, 5 and 6 along with the associated weights to determine the group decision matrix. The resulting group decision matrix is presented in Table 7. For this aggregation process, we utilized \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:(p,q,r)-$$\end{document}SFFWA operator as presented in Eq. (18).

Collective decision matrix.

After thoroughly analyzing the data presented in Tables 4, 5 and 6, we have determined that the optimal values for the parameters are \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:p=3$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:q=2$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:r=\text{max}\left(p,q\right)=\text{max}\left(\text{3,2}\right)=3$$\end{document}. With these chosen values, each entry in the dataset adheres to the condition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\left({\mathcal{U}}_{ij}^{x}\right)}^{p}+{\left({\mathcal{E}}_{ij}^{x}\right)}^{r}+{\left({\mathcal{V}}_{ij}^{x}\right)}^{q}\le\:1$$\end{document}. This ensures that our parameter selection is consistent with the constraints imposed by the data, providing a robust foundation for our decision-making model.

We used Eqs. (15)-(17) to calculate the weights for each criterion. As a result, the weight vector for the criteria is determined to be \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:(0.2432,\:0.2347,\:0.2716,\:0.2505)$$\end{document}. This vector reflects the relative importance of each criterion in the decision-making process, ensuring that our evaluations are balanced and aligned with our predefined priorities. Table 7 and the criteria weights were utilized to compute the weighted decision matrix, which is presented in Table 8.

Preference values of the alternatives.

Equations (21) and (22) were employed to calculate the score and accuracy values for each alternative. Based on these score values, the ranking order of the renewable energy alternatives is as follows: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\Xi\:}}_{3}>{{\Xi\:}}_{4}>{{\Xi\:}}_{1}>{{\Xi\:}}_{2}$$\end{document}.

This ranking indicates that Solar Energy (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\Xi\:}}_{3}$$\end{document}) is the most preferred option, followed by Biomass Energy (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\Xi\:}}_{4}$$\end{document}), Hydro Energy (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\Xi\:}}_{1}$$\end{document}), and finally Wind Energy (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{{\Xi\:}}_{2}$$\end{document}). This order reflects the comprehensive evaluation of each alternative against the criteria and the weights assigned to those criteria. Figure 6 represents the graphical representation of score values and ranking order.

The influence of parameters \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\varvec{p}, \:\varvec{q} {\rm and} \:\varvec{r}$$\end{document}

In the above investigation, we used the values of parameters \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:p=3$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:q=2$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:r=3$$\end{document}. It is evident that the condition \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\left({\mathcal{U}}_{ij}\right)}^{p}+{\left({\mathcal{E}}_{ij}\right)}^{r}+{\left({\mathcal{V}}_{ij}\right)}^{q}\le\:1$$\end{document} is satisfied when we fix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:p=3$$\end{document} and vary values of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:q$$\end{document} (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:q\ge\:2$$\end{document}). Score values of alternatives for different values of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:p$$\end{document} while fixing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:q=2$$\end{document}.

Tables 10 and 11 demonstrate that the proposed AOs are symmetric concerning the parameters \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:p$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:q$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:r$$\end{document}. These tables show that while altering these parameters changes the score values of the aggregated results, the ranking of the alternatives remains unchanged. This stability is essential for practical decision-making. For example, it has been noted that increasing the parameters typically results in higher score values for the alternatives, offering a more optimistic perspective for decision-makers. Therefore, optimistic decision-makers may assign higher values to these parameters during the aggregation process. Conversely, pessimistic decision-makers may assign lower values, resulting in reduced score values. Nonetheless, the top-ranked alternative remains constant, indicating that the results are objective and not influenced by the decision-makers’ level of optimism or pessimism. This consistency ensures the reliability of the ranking results. Figure 7 provides a graphical representation of score values for different values of parameter \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:p$$\end{document}.

The influence of parameter Frank \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\varvec{\rho\:}$$\end{document}

In the aggregation process, the Frank parameter plays a pivotal role by offering a versatile framework for incorporating dependency structures and decision maker preferences. This parameter can be utilized in various ways, such as adjusting the influence of different criteria or decision makers through weighting schemes, modeling interdependence within the aggregation framework, and facilitating trade-off analysis by quantifying the trade-off between conflicting objectives. Sensitivity analysis involving the Frank parameter allows decision makers to assess the robustness of aggregation results to changes in dependency structures or preferences, while its integration into scoring systems enables the prioritization of consensus or diversity of opinions among decision makers.

the graphical view of score values for different values of parameter \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:q$$\end{document}.

To investigate the influence of the Frank parameter \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\rho\:$$\end{document}, we examined the outcomes of the proposed decision-making model with different values of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\rho\:$$\end{document}. The results for various values of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\rho\:$$\end{document} are presented in Table 12. From this table, we observe that the score values decrease as the values of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\rho\:$$\end{document} increase. This observation is closely tied to the real-world implications of the parameter \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\rho\:$$\end{document} in the context of MAGDM, specifically indicating the risk preferences of decision makers, as underscored by Tang et al.59.

Ranking order of alternative with different values of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\rho\:$$\end{document} (where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:p=r=3$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:q=2$$\end{document})

The Frank parameter provides significant advantages for the proposed case study on the classification of renewable energy sources by enhancing the modeling of dependencies between criteria such as cost, environmental impact, reliability, and scalability. It offers flexibility in reflecting the risk preferences of decision makers, allowing for adjustments that align with their risk attitudes. This parameter improves the robustness of DM outcomes by managing the influence of extreme values and outliers, ensuring stable and reliable rankings. Additionally, it facilitates consensus building by balancing conflicting opinions and supports comprehensive sensitivity analysis to understand the impact of parameter changes on rankings. The analytical rigor provided by the Frank parameter enhances the credibility and acceptance of results, making the DM process transparent and scientifically sound. Its applicability across various scenarios ensures that all relevant factors are considered, contributing to accurate, reliable, and acceptable DM outcomes for sustainable energy development.

Comparative study

The existing approaches under the picture fuzzy52,60,61 and spherical fuzzy54,62, environments are inadequate for handling the data presented in Tables 1, 2 and 3. This limitation arises due to the constraints imposed on these sets, specifically \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\left({\mathcal{U}}_{ij}\right)}^{1}+{\left({\mathcal{E}}_{ij}\right)}^{1}+{\left({\mathcal{V}}_{ij}\right)}^{1}\le\:1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\left({\mathcal{U}}_{ij}\right)}^{2}+{\left({\mathcal{E}}_{ij}\right)}^{2}+{\left({\mathcal{V}}_{ij}\right)}^{2}\le\:1$$\end{document}. Consequently, we validate our proposed approach by comparing it with several methods utilizing T-spherical fuzzy sets63–65] and [66, which offer a more flexible framework for dealing with complex and interdependent criteria in DM scenarios. This comparison helps demonstrate the effectiveness and applicability of our model in addressing the shortcomings of traditional fuzzy environments. The Score values and ranking order of alternatives with some approaches are summarized in Table 13. The graphical view of ranking order is presented in Fig. 8.

Comparison.

This investigation demonstrates that the ranking order of the alternatives aligns closely with the ranking order achieved using the proposed approach, indicating a strong correlation between the results of our method and those derived from established techniques. This consistency not only confirms the validity and reliability of our proposed method but also underscores its robustness in various decision-making scenarios. The parameters \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:p$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:q$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:r\:$$\end{document}play a crucial role in this process by allowing for a more nuanced and flexible management of membership, neutral, and non-membership degrees, respectively. By adjusting these parameters, decision-makers can tailor the aggregation process to better reflect the specific uncertainties and preferences inherent in the problem at hand. This adaptability enhances the overall precision of the decision-making process, ensuring that the selected alternatives are not only consistent with traditional approaches but also optimally aligned with the specific criteria and objectives of the analysis. The advantages of the proposed approach are summarized as follows.

Advantages of the proposed approach

The proposed study allows decision-makers to adjust the influence of criteria dynamically, accommodating changing conditions and preferences. For instance, if the cost of a particular renewable energy source increases, decision-makers can modify the weight assigned to the cost criterion and reassess their evaluations accordingly. This flexibility ensures that the model remains relevant and accurate in varying contexts (see “The influence of parameters p, q and r” and “The influence of parameter Frank ρ”).By using t-spherical fuzzy sets, the proposed approach overcomes the restrictive constraints of traditional fuzzy environments (such as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\left({\mathcal{U}}_{ij}\right)}^{1}+{\left({\mathcal{E}}_{ij}\right)}^{1}+{\left({\mathcal{V}}_{ij}\right)}^{1}\le\:1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:{\left({\mathcal{U}}_{ij}\right)}^{2}+{\left({\mathcal{E}}_{ij}\right)}^{2}+{\left({\mathcal{V}}_{ij}\right)}^{2}\le\:1$$\end{document}). This leads to a more precise representation of complex interdependencies among criteria, resulting in more accurate and reliable evaluations (see “Comparative study”). The robustness of this approach ensures consistent and dependable ranking of renewable energy sources.The integration of the Frank parameter \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\rho\:$$\end{document} enables the model to reflect the risk preferences of decision-makers effectively. Decision-makers who are risk-averse can assign lower values to the parameter, while those who are more risk-tolerant can assign higher values (see Table 12). This adaptability ensures that the aggregation process aligns with the specific risk attitudes of the decision-makers, providing a more tailored decision-making framework.The proposed study supports thorough sensitivity analysis by allowing for the exploration of how changes in parameter values affect the final rankings of alternatives. This capability helps in understanding the robustness of the results and identifying the most influential criteria. It aids decision-makers in making informed adjustments and enhances confidence in the final decision outcomes (see subsections “The influence of parameters p, q and r”, “The influence of parameter Frank ρ” and “Comparative study”).The model facilitates better consensus among decision-makers by providing a structured method for aggregating diverse opinions and preferences. This structured approach helps balance conflicting viewpoints and find a middle ground that is acceptable to all participants, improving overall agreement and satisfaction with the final decision (see “Proposed MCGDM approach” and “Case study”).

The integration of the Frank parameter \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\rho\:$$\end{document} enables the model to reflect the risk preferences of decision-makers effectively. The integration of the Frank parameter \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\rho\:$$\end{document} enables the model to reflect the risk preferences of decision-makers effectively. The proposed study supports thorough sensitivity analysis by allowing for the exploration of how changes in parameter values affect the final rankings of alternatives. This capability helps in understanding the robustness of the results and identifying the most influential criteria. It aids decision-makers in making informed adjustments and enhances confidence in the final decision outcomes (see subsections “The influence of parameters p, q and r”, “The influence of parameter Frank ρ” and “Comparative study”).

This capability helps in understanding the robustness of the results and identifying the most influential criteria. It aids decision-makers in making informed adjustments and enhances confidence in the final decision outcomes (see subsections “The influence of parameters p, q and r”, “The influence of parameter Frank ρ” and “Comparative study”).

The model facilitates better consensus among decision-makers by providing a structured method for aggregating diverse opinions and preferences. This structured approach helps balance conflicting viewpoints and find a middle ground that is acceptable to all participants, improving overall agreement and satisfaction with the final decision (see “Proposed MCGDM approach” and “Case study”).

Limitations

Despite the strengths of the proposed approach, there are some limitations to consider. First, the reliance on multiple parameters (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:p$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:q$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:r$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\rho\:$$\end{document}) introduces added complexity to the decision-making process, which may require extensive expertise and careful calibration to ensure optimal outcomes. Additionally, the computational burden associated with the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:(p,q,r)-$$\end{document}Spherical Fuzzy Frank aggregation operators can increase significantly with the scale of the problem, potentially limiting their practicality in large-scale applications. Finally, while the proposed method has shown effectiveness in the context of renewable energy classification, its applicability to other domains or decision-making scenarios may require further validation and adaptation to account for different types of uncertainties or decision-making criteria.

The comparison of alternatives with different existing approaches.

Conclusion

In this paper, we have introduced novel operational laws for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:(p,q,r)-$$\end{document}Spherical Fuzzy Sets (SFSs) utilizing the Frank norm and t-conorm, and we developed two aggregation operators: the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:(p,q,r)-$$\end{document}Spherical Fuzzy Frank Weighted Averaging (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:(p,q,r)-$$\end{document}SFFWA) and the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:(p,q,r)-$$\end{document}Spherical Fuzzy Frank Weighted Geometric (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:(p,q,r)-$$\end{document}SFFWG) operators. These operators form the foundation of a multi-criteria group decision-making (MCGDM) method designed to address complex real-world decision-making problems. The effectiveness of our proposed approach is illustrated through a case study on the classification of renewable energy sources, demonstrating its practical applicability. The inclusion of four parameters \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:p$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:q$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:r$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\:\rho\:$$\end{document} provides significant flexibility in modeling decision-makers’ preferences and managing uncertainty. We conducted a thorough analysis of how variations in these parameters influence the decision-making process, ensuring the robustness and reliability of the results. A comparative analysis with existing methods reveals that our approach offers enhanced flexibility, greater accuracy, and improved consensus-building capabilities among decision-makers.

Looking forward, future research could explore the extension of these operational laws and aggregation operators to decision-making frameworks. Additionally, applying the proposed methodology to a broader range of practical scenarios, such as environmental management or healthcare decision-making, could further validate its versatility and effectiveness. We also plan to investigate the potential integration of machine learning techniques to automate parameter selection and enhance the decision-making process in more dynamic and complex environments.
